# Canned mushrooms

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="canned-mushrooms-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="canned-mushrooms-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="canned-mushrooms-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Canned mushrooms in the HMI library has 54 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn from 7 sources.<sup ## Canned mushrooms overview

<a id="which-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-ingredient-and-its-defined-forms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-005"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Canned mushrooms in the HMI library has 54 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn from 7 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in canned mushrooms, as sold from 55 results in 2 reporting regions, with subgroup medians below 13 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, canned mushrooms, canned had a median lead result below 4 µg/kg across 1 composite from 2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Dabeka et al. 1995 reported lead at 71.6 µg/kg (median) in canned mushrooms, canned from 5 city composites.<sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Spungen et al. 2024 reported cadmium at 1.8 µg/kg (mean) in canned mushrooms, canned from all samples.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the canned mushrooms came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

## What counts as canned mushrooms

<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-canned-mushrooms" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-canned-mushrooms-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of canned mushrooms, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Canned mushrooms, canned; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>2 reference units; 2 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>1</td><td>50%</td></tr><tr><td>Spungen et al. 2024</td><td>1</td><td>50%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for canned mushrooms, canned on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>2 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li><li><a href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" class="internal" data-slug="sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" data-no-cite-meta>Spungen et al. 2024</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median &lt;4 µg/kg (below reporting limits)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>1 composites. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI canned mushrooms Pb reference · Canned mushrooms, canned. median as reported: ≤4 µg/kg. HMI median ≤4; Q1 ≤4; Q3 ≤4 µg/kg. 2 families. Too few reference units.</p><p>Only 2 reference unit is available; at least four are required by this descriptive display rule.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without FDA Total Diet Study: Limited data.</li><li>Without Spungen et al. 2024: Limited data.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:canned-mushrooms-02a0dca674c1:778731329a70"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:canned-mushrooms-02a0dca674c1:778731329a70">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2020</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>1 collection regions</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>median</td><td>0 of 1 detected; HMI median of individual results</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>1 dataset rows, for example Original FDA TSV row 25922 (header = row 1).</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. Any basis conversion used for its HMI comparison is stated separately with the numerical target.</p></details><p><a href="https://www.fda.gov/food/fda-total-diet-study-tds/fda-total-diet-study-tds-results">Open the primary publication</a> · <a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">HMI source record</a></p></div><div data-evidence-content><p class="hmi-dataset-title"><a href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals" class="internal" data-slug="sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Korfali et al. 2013</a></p><p>Canned mushrooms, canned · Lead (Pb) · Sample count unreported.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">124 µg/kg (maximum)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">max.</span><p>Sample count unreported. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>No censoring reported</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data: A max describes spread or an extreme, not a central value that can be placed against a reference. Missing evidence never means low contamination.</p><p>A max describes spread or an extreme, not a central value that can be placed against a reference.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>No admitted reference</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>Collection date unreported</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>mushroom, highest level</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Variety within one study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>Drained, homogenized; H2O2/HNO3 digestion; GF-AAS for Cd, Cr, Cu, Fe, Pb, Zn; ICP-MS for Al, Hg, Sn; handheld XRF for cans.</p><p>No censoring reported</p><p>Single value stated in the text; the paper does not say whether it is one can or a mean of cans unless worded as mean. Per-sample bar labels in Figures 1-2 are not recorded.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>max</td><td>0.124 µg/g</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>Results text, p. 26.</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach canned mushrooms

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared canned mushrooms from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

The HMI reference for lead in canned mushrooms, as sold pools 8 subgroup units from 1 sampling family, with a median of between 1.27 and 3.65 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 1.77 (lower quartile) and between 3.65 and 12.3 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For total arsenic in canned mushrooms, as sold, the reference median was between 4.13 and 7.22 µg/kg, with a middle half from between 3.55 and 5.3 to between 7 and 13 µg/kg across 10 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

In one study, lead across 6 groups of canned mushrooms ranged from 42.7 µg/kg (Halifax 1986) to 106 µg/kg (Toronto 1988); the lowest is the lowest observed in that study, not an established sourcing preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>


<a id="processing-effects"></a>
<a id="ingredient-derivative-risk"></a>
<a id="processing-effects-and-derivatives"></a>
## Processing, cooking and preparation

<a id="hmi-ing-016"></a>
<a id="how-do-specified-processing-or-preparation-steps-change-the-measured-metal-endpoints"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-017"></a>
<a id="how-do-derivatives-concentrates-extracts-and-fractions-differ-from-their-parent-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-018"></a>
<a id="do-changes-reflect-removal-redistribution-moisture-or-yield-change-dilution-or-altered-species-or-availability"></a>

No processing study for metals in this food is in the library yet.



<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for canned mushrooms

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing canned mushrooms for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 90 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium were 1 µg/kg where sources stated them.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for total arsenic ranged from 3 µg/kg (FDA Total Diet Study) to 50 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.



<a id="procurement-and-process-control"></a>
## Buying and verifying canned mushrooms

<a id="hmi-ing-028"></a>
<a id="what-origin-traceability-processing-and-analytical-information-should-a-buyer-request"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-029"></a>
<a id="which-control-or-specification-choices-are-supported-and-which-are-policy-choices-requiring-separate-justification"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-030"></a>
<a id="what-supplier-process-or-test-result-changes-should-trigger-investigation-or-requalification"></a>

No maximum level for metals in canned mushrooms was verified from primary text for this page; buyers should confirm the limit that applies in the destination market.



<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from canned mushrooms

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-032"></a>
<a id="how-do-consumption-patterns-and-intended-populations-affect-dietary-exposure-interpretation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-033"></a>
<a id="what-conclusions-require-finished-product-measurements-or-a-separate-exposure-assessment"></a>

No source in the library converted canned mushrooms concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-036"></a>
<a id="what-remains-unresolved-when-was-each-answer-reviewed-and-what-changed-in-this-version"></a>

Every source record that names canned mushrooms was decided on its content: 7 occurrence extracted, 7 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Canned mushrooms FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-canned-mushrooms" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-canned-mushrooms" data-question="What heavy metals are found in canned mushrooms?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in canned mushrooms?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn in canned mushrooms, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Korfali SI, Abou Hamdan W · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Essentail and Toxic Metals in Lebanese Marketed Canned Food: Impact of Metal Cans</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Food Research 2(1) (2013) 19-30</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in canned mushrooms, as sold from 55 results in 2 reporting regions, with subgroup medians below 13 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-origins-or-products-of-canned-mushrooms-measured-lowest" data-anchor="which-origins-or-products-of-canned-mushrooms-measured-lowest" data-question="Which origins or products of canned mushrooms measured lowest?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which origins or products of canned mushrooms measured lowest?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In one study, lead across 6 groups of canned mushrooms ranged from 42.7 µg/kg (Halifax 1986) to 106 µg/kg (Toronto 1988); the lowest is the lowest observed in that study, not an established sourcing preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dabeka RW, McKenzie AD · 1995</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Survey of Lead, Cadmium, Fluoride, Nickel, and Cobalt in Food Composites and Estimation of Dietary Intakes of These Elements by Canadians in 1986-1988</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of AOAC International</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dabeka1995-canadian-food-composites-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
